      "raw_content": "[History](#1)[Administrative Divisions](#2)[Overview](#2-1)[Detailed Introduction](#2-2)[Geography and Environment](#3)[Economy](#4)[Overview](#4-1)[Industrial Strength](#4-2)[Strength of Agriculture and Animal Husbandry](#4-3)[Strength of the Service Sector](#4-4)[Major Ports](#4-5)[Urban Charm](#5)[Carnival](#5-1)[Samba](#5-2)[Carnival](#5-3)[Football Charm](#5-4)[City Stories](#6)[Transportation](#7)[City Culture](#8)[Cultural Events](#8-1)[Sports](#8-2)[Culinary Culture](#9)[Tourist Attractions](#10)[Beaches](#10-1)[Sugarloaf Mountain](#10-2)[Corcovado Mountain (Jesus Mountain)](#10-3)[Christ the Redeemer Statue](#10-4)[National Museum of Brazil](#10-5)[Shopping Centers](#10-6)[Best Time to Visit](#10-7)[Important Notes](#10-8)[Leisure and Entertainment](#10-9)[Travel Tips](#10-10)[Sister cities](#11)[Real-time News](#12)[Bid Process](#13)\n\nKey Information\n\n# Rio de Janeiro\n\nthe second largest city in Brazil\n\n![](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/34fae6cd7b899e510fb367ad26fbce33c895d143a74c/34fae6cd7b899e510fb367ad26fbce33c895d143a74c_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,w_400,limit_1)\n\n![](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/960a304e251f95cad1c811a1204f683e6709c93df429/960a304e251f95cad1c811a1204f683e6709c93df429_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,w_400,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro (Portuguese: Rio de Janeiro, meaning \"January River\"), abbreviated as Rio, is located on the Atlantic coast of southeastern Brazil . It is the capital of the state of Rio de Janeiro and the second-largest city in Brazil. It covers an area of 1,204 square kilometers and has a population of 6.52 million.\n\nThe city was founded in 1565 and served as the capital of Brazilfrom 1763 to 1960. It has a tropical savanna climate and possesses a world-famous natural harbor. In 2012, its unique landscape was inscribed as a World Cultural Heritage site by UNESCO.\n\nAs a major economic center in República Federativa do Brasil and South America, Rio is renowned as a transportation hub and for its finance and tourism sectors. Famous landmarks include the Christ the Redeemer statue and the Maracanã Stadium . The city is famous for its Carnival and Samba and hosted the 2014 FIFA World Cup and the 2016 Summer Olympics.\n\nChinese Name\n\nAirport\n:   Rio de Janeiro International Airport\n\nAlias\n\nRailway Station\n:   Rio de Janeiro Railway Station\n\nAdministrat­ive Category\n\nTime Zone\n:   UTC-3\n\nBelonging Region\n\nSignificant Events\n:   Hosted the 31st Summer Olympic Games in 2016\n\n    13\n\nGeographic Location\n:   On the Atlantic coast in southeastern Brazil\n\nPopulation\n:   6.52 million\n\n    13\n\nSubordinate Regions\n:   State Capital District\n\nDialect\n:   Portuguese\n\n    13\n\nTelephone Area Code\n:   Country Code: +55 Area Code: +21\n\nGeographic Coordinates\n:   22°54′30″S 43°11′47″W\n\n    13\n\nPostal Code\n:   999074\n\nPopulation\n:   6.3 million\n\n    13\n\nClimate\n:   Tropical savanna climate (partially tropical rainforest climate)\n\nArea\n:   1,204 square kilometres\n\n    13\n\nFamous Attractions\n:   Corcovado Mountain, Sugarloaf Mountain, Rio–Niterói Bridge, Maracanã Stadium, etc.\n\nHistory\n\nRio de Janeiro (Rio for short) is the largest seaport in the Federative Republic of Brazil, located on the western shore of the Atlantic Ocean in the southeastern part of the Federative Republic of Brazil. It is the capital of the state of Rio de Janeiro and, after São Paulo, the second largest city in Brazil. Covering an area of 1,204 square kilometers and with a population of 6.52 million, it is known as the \"Carnival Capital\" in the Federative Republic of Brazil.\n\n1\n\n![Location of Rio in Brazil](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/ca1349540923dd547908e251d209b3de9c82488b/ca1349540923dd547908e251d209b3de9c82488b_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nLocation of Rio in Brazil\n\nRio de Janeiro means \"River of January\" in Portuguese , named after Portuguese explorers who arrived there in January 1505. The city's construction began 60 years later. It served as the capital of Brazil from 1763 to 1960. In April 1960, the Brazilian government moved the capital to Brasilia . However, a significant number of Federal Government agencies, as well as headquarters of associations and companies, are still located here, hence it is also called Brazil's \"second capital\".\n\nIn Rio de Janeiro, well-preserved ancient buildings can be seen everywhere. Most of them have been converted into memorial halls or museums. Brazil's National Museum is one of the most famous major museums in the world today, housing a collection of over one million items.\n\nRio de Janeiro is a world-renowned tourist destination. It has over 30 beaches with a total length of 200 kilometers. The most famous among them, Copacabana Beach, is known for its white sand and clean water, forming a crescent shape that stretches for 8 kilometers. According to statistics, nearly 40% of the over 2 million tourists who visit Brazil annually come to this city.\n\nRio de Janeiro is also Brazil's second-largest industrial city and its largest commercial center. Its Gross value of industrial output accounts for approximately 15% of the nation's total. Industries such as textiles, clothing, chemicals, pharmaceuticals, printing and publishing, and metallurgy all hold important positions nationally.\n\n![Christ the Redeemer Statue](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/cc11728b4710b912d46866a5c4fdfc039245223b/cc11728b4710b912d46866a5c4fdfc039245223b_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nChrist the Redeemer Statue\n\nOn October 2, 2009, Rio de Janeiro won the bid to host the 2016 Summer Olympics. On July 1, 2012, at the 36th session of the World Heritage Committee held in Saint Petersburg , Russia, the unique landscape of Rio de Janeiro was inscribed as a World Cultural Heritage site, making Rio the first city to have its natural landscape category selected as a World Cultural Heritage.\n\nOn January 20, 1502, Portuguese explorer Pedro Álvares Cabral discovered Guanabara Bay, the location of the present-day city, and named the place Rio de Janeiro, meaning \"January River\". At the time, they believed the bay was actually a river mouth. In fact, \"river\" was a common term for any large body of water at that time. Subsequently, French fleets also arrived, using it as a base for smuggling Brazilian timber. In 1555, French Navy officer Nicolas Durand de Villegaignon led over 600 soldiers and colonists to establish the first European colony here, named \"France Antarctique\". The colonists were primarily composed of French Huguenots and Swiss Confederation Calvinists.\n\nThe city was later founded on March 1, 1565, under the invasion of Portuguese colonists. They successfully captured the city and Deportation the French. Thereafter, the city was frequently attacked by pirates and privateers, especially the French. Therefore, starting in the 16th century, the Portuguese began constructing fortresses here for defense and to resist invaders.\n\nThe city continued to serve as the colonial capital until 1808. At that time, fearing an invasion of Portugal by Napoleon Bonaparte , Emperor of the French Empire, the Portuguese nobility and royal family fled to Rio de Janeiro. The city even became the capital of Portugal, making Rio de Janeiro the only city outside Europe to serve as the capital of a European country. At that time, due to the sudden migration of the nobility, the city lacked adequate construction space to accommodate them. Consequently, a large number of original residents were forced to relocate to make room for the Portuguese nobles.\n\nIn 1822, Pedro I , the first emperor of Brazil, declared Brazil's independence and decided to make Rio de Janeiro the national capital. However, the city gradually lost importance as he shifted the economic and political center to São Paulo. Yet, when the Monarchy was replaced by a republic in 1889, Rio de Janeiro remained the capital of Brazil. It was also during this period that a large number of Africans were brought to Rio de Janeiro as slaves. By 1840, the number of slaves in the city had reached 220,000. In its early years, Rio de Janeiro's population was concentrated in what is today the central business district. It was not until the early 20th century that the city began to develop rapidly, and the city's center of gravity started shifting southward and westward.\n\nAs early as the 1930s, the Brazilian government had begun discussions on relocating the capital. In 1955, the newly inaugurated President Juscelino Kubitschek vigorously promoted the construction of a new capital. Despite many opinions viewing this as merely an election-driven move, Kubitschek ultimately succeeded in moving the capital to Brasíliaon April 21, 1960. Rio de Janeiro was then downgraded to an ordinary city.\n\n![Overlooking Rio](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/3c6d55fbb2fb43163581920922a4462309f7d323/3c6d55fbb2fb43163581920922a4462309f7d323_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nOverlooking Rio\n\nOn October 2, 2009, Rio de Janeiro was awarded the hosting rights for the 31st Summer Olympic Games in 2016.\n\nThe cityscape of Rio is unique, where the beautiful natural scenery of mountains and sea blending together is harmoniously integrated with Cultural Landscape . The main attractions in Rio include Tijuca National Park, Corcovado Mountain, the Botanical Garden founded in 1808, Guanabara Bay, and Copacabana Beach, among others. These landscapes have contributed significantly to the local outdoor living culture and have served as a source of artistic inspiration for musicians, landscape architects, and urban planning experts.\n\n1\n\n![Bird's-eye view of Rio](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/d62a6059252dd42a7de6c2e9043b5bb5c8eab881/d62a6059252dd42a7de6c2e9043b5bb5c8eab881_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nBird's-eye view of Rio\n\nAdministrative Divisions\n\nOverview\n\nThe state of Rio de Janeiro is divided into 8 regions (Regioes), comprising a total of 92 municipalities (Municipios). The brief details of these 8 regions are as follows:\n\n![Administrative Divisions of Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/90cebeec94262e6227979119/90cebeec94262e6227979119_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nAdministrative Divisions of Rio de Janeiro\n\n1. The Metropolitan Region (Regiao Metropolitana). This region is located along the southeastern coast of Rio de Janeiro State. It covers an area of 5,693.4 km², accounting for 12.97% of the state's total area. Its population is 10,348,002, representing 71.84% of the state's total population, making it the most densely populated area in the state. It consists of 20 municipalities, including the city of Rio de Janeiro. The region concentrates the state's main ports and is primarily focused on tourism, commerce, and finance.\n\n2. The Coastal Lowlands Region (Regiao das Baixadas Litoraneas). This region is located along the eastern coast of Rio de Janeiro State, adjacent to the southwest of the Metropolitan Region. It covers an area of 5,064 km² with a population of 503,151. It consists of 12 municipalities and is primarily focused on the petrochemical industry.\n\n![Satellite Map of Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/622762d0f703918fa0ec50f7ec75319759ee3d6d6eff/622762d0f703918fa0ec50f7ec75319759ee3d6d6eff_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nSatellite Map of Rio de Janeiro\n\n3. The Northern Region (Regiao Norte). This region is located along the northeastern coast of Rio de Janeiro State, bordering the Coastal Lowlands Region to the south. It covers an area of 9,767.8 km² with a population of 671,400. Its 9 municipalities are mainly home to petrochemical industrial complexes and alcoholic beverage industries.\n\n4. The Northwestern Region (Regiao Noroeste). Located in the northwestern part of Rio de Janeiro State, it borders the Northern Region to the east. It covers an area of 5,385.4 km² with a population of 287,946. It consists of 13 municipalities and is primarily focused on agricultural product processing.\n\n5. The Ilha Grande Bay Region (Regiao da Baia da Ilha Grande). Located at the southernmost tip of Rio de Janeiro State, it covers an area of 1,753.4 km² with a population of 123,855. It consists of only two municipalities: Angra dos Reis and Parati. It is primarily focused on tourism and nuclear power generation.\n\n![Satellite Image of Rio de Janeiro City](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/346bd85c56dc5572faf2c032/346bd85c56dc5572faf2c032_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nSatellite Image of Rio de Janeiro City\n\n6. Médio Paraíba Region (Região do Médio Paraíba), located in the southwestern part of the state, has an area of 6,203 km², a population of 759,998, comprises 12 municipalities, and is primarily focused on metallurgy and metal mechanical processing.\n\n7. Centro-Sul Fluminense Region (Região Centro-Sul Fluminense), bordering the Metropolitan Region to the east, has an area of 3,036 km², a population of 236,926, consists of 10 municipalities, and is primarily focused on tourism and the garment processing industry.\n\n8. Serrana Region (Região Serrana), located north of the Centro-Sul Fluminense Region, has an area of 6,960.6 km² and a population of 722,334. It comprises 14 municipalities and is primarily focused on tourism and the garment processing industry.\n\nGenerally speaking, Rio de Janeiro can be divided into three major areas: the city center (Central Zone), the South Zone which is convenient for foreign tourists and boasts world-famous beaches, the North Zone with concentrated factories, and the West Zone consisting of new towns.\n\nDetailed Introduction\n\nCity Center\n\nDowntown (Centro) is the city's financial center. Historic sites in the area include the Paço Imperial, built during the Portuguese colonial period and used as the residence for the Governor-General of Brazil; as well as many historic churches, such as the Candelária Church (meaning Church of Our Lady of Candelária), the Old Cathedral of Rio de Janeiro, and the modern Cathedral of Rio de Janeiro. Near Cinelndia Square (meaning Theatre Square) are several of the city's well-known landmarks, including the Municipal Theatre of Rio de Janeiro and the National Library of Brazil. Among the museums, the National Museum of Fine Arts and the National Historical Museum are the most representative.\n\n![Downtown Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/9825bc315c6034a854921dd9c913495409237671/9825bc315c6034a854921dd9c913495409237671_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nDowntown Rio de Janeiro\n\nDowntown is the commercial heart of the city, where the largest Brazilian companies have their headquarters, including Petrobras and Vale (two of Brazil's largest companies).\n\nSouth Zone\n\nIpanema Beach, located in the South Zone.\n\nThe South Zone of Rio de Janeiro comprises several districts, encompassing the city's beachfront. The New Year's Eve party, Reveillon, held annually at Copacabana Beach, is one of the most spectacular parties in the world, with over two million revelers flocking to the beach to watch the fireworks display. Since 2001, the fireworks have been launched from boats to enhance the safety of the show.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro Beachfront](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/f636afc379310a55d7d57facb54543a9832610f0/f636afc379310a55d7d57facb54543a9832610f0_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro Beachfront\n\nThe South Zone is home to the Maracanã Stadium, which was once the stadium with the largest spectator capacity in the world and hosted the 1950 FIFA World Cup. In modern times, due to safety regulations, the stadium's capacity has been significantly reduced and it now accommodates 95,000 spectators. The Maracanã was the venue for the opening and closing ceremonies and the football matches of the 2007 Pan American Games.\n\n![Maracanã Stadium](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/8644ebf81a4c510f6e9743426259252dd42aa513/8644ebf81a4c510f6e9743426259252dd42aa513_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nMaracanã Stadium\n\nWest Zone\n\nThe West Zone is the most recently developed area of Rio de Janeiro. The entire zone is predominantly composed of upscale residential areas, but also contains shantytowns.\n\n![West Zone Peninsula, Venue for the 2016 Rio de Janeiro Olympics](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/203fb80e7bec54e7d83bbf46bb389b504ec26a97/203fb80e7bec54e7d83bbf46bb389b504ec26a97_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nWest Zone Peninsula, Venue for the 2016 Rio de Janeiro Olympics\n\nThe overall planning of the area is similar to the suburban regions of some American cities, but it primarily features high-end residential towers, attracting a wealthy population, and includes luxurious shopping centers. There is also undeveloped land along the coastal strip.\n\nThis zone was the competition venue for the 2016 Summer Olympics and the location of the Olympic Village.\n\nGeography and Environment\n\nRio de Janeiro is Brazil's second-largest city and its largest seaport. It is located in the southeastern part of Brazil, bordering the Atlantic Ocean to the south, on the western shore of Guanabara Bay, which extends northward. A 14-kilometer cross-bay highway bridge connects it with Niterói City on the eastern shore. The city covers an area of 1,204 square kilometers and is 2.3 meters above sea level. It is 35 kilometers long from north to south and 70 kilometers from east to west. Green space covers 325.6 square kilometers, and the population is 6.52 million. The annual mean temperature ranges from 17 to 36 degrees Celsius. In January 1502, Portuguese navigators arrived at the bay and, mistaking it for the mouth of a large river, casually named it \"January River\", which in Portuguese is \"Rio de Janeiro\". The city was founded in 1565. It became the capital of Brazil in 1763 and the national capital in 1822; in 1960, the capital was moved from here to Brasília . Later it became Guanabara State (with the same boundaries as the city), and after Guanabara State was abolished in 1975, it became the capital of the state of Rio de Janeiro. It is the country's economic and cultural center. It has textile, clothing, printing, automobile, shipbuilding, machinery, petroleum processing, chemical, metallurgical, and food industries. It is the country's largest financial center; many large enterprises, banks, and monopoly organizations have their management offices here. The city has more than 60 museums of various types, over 70 libraries, 85 theaters of all sizes, and 82 clubs. It also has many institutions of higher learning, scientific research institutions, and so on. It has well-developed land, sea, and air transportation. It is Brazil's largest port. Its harbor, broad inside and narrow at the mouth, can berth huge ships and has an annual throughput of over 35 million tons. It is a world-famous tourist center. Located on the beautiful Guanabara Bay and nestled between mountains and water, it has beautiful scenery and is a famous tourist destination in Brazil and the world. Its main attractions include Corcovado, Sugarloaf Mountain, the Rio-Niterói Bridge, Maracanã Stadium , Brazil's largest park, and the botanical garden, among others. The beaches of Rio de Janeiro are world-renowned, ranking first in the world in number and total length. The city has a total of 72 beaches, of which the two most famous are Copacabana Beach and Ipanema Beach. The best months for tourism are June and July every year.\n\n19\n\nBrazil is a kingdom of football, with football clubs spread throughout the country, and Rio de Janeiro is no exception. Four teams from Rio de Janeiro compete in the Brazilian Série A: Flamengo, Botafogo, Vasco da Gama, and Fluminense.\n\n![Brazil World Cup](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/c8ea15ce36d3d5397f15b59b3887e950352ab0ba/c8ea15ce36d3d5397f15b59b3887e950352ab0ba_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nBrazil World Cup\n\nBesides the city of Rio, other areas within Rio de Janeiro State boast 28 state-famous attractions and tourist scenic spots that are a source of pride for both the state and Brazil. Fourteen of these are distributed along the coastal region, mostly consisting of picturesque bays, beaches, and lush tropical islands.\n\nThe coastal lowlands have a hot and humid climate, while the inland plateau has a temperate climate. Important rivers include the Paraíba River, among others.\n\nThe eastern part lies on the windward slope of the southeast trade winds, receiving more precipitation. Influenced by the coastal warm current, which has warming and moistening effects, the area receives more precipitation than the inland plateau.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/30adcbef76094b36fbc532e7a1cc7cd98d109d60/30adcbef76094b36fbc532e7a1cc7cd98d109d60_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/9e3df8dcd100baa1ce74cdc94510b912c8fc2e63/9e3df8dcd100baa1ce74cdc94510b912c8fc2e63_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro\n\n![Carnival](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/4afbfbedab64034f1ce2785eadc379310a551dbe/4afbfbedab64034f1ce2785eadc379310a551dbe_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nCarnival\n\n![Paraíba River](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/5ab5c9ea15ce36d31d22396838f33a87e950b140/5ab5c9ea15ce36d31d22396838f33a87e950b140_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nParaíba River\n\nEconomy\n\nOverview\n\nIn 2000, the Gross Domestic Product (GDP) of Rio de Janeiro State was 82.3 billion US dollars (162.6 billion reais), accounting for 14.36% of Brazil's total GDP, with a per capita figure of 5,734 US dollars. In 1999, the state's GDP was 109.6 billion US dollars, representing 13.53% of Brazil's GDP. Of this, agriculture and Livestock accounted for 0.6%, industry for 29.3%, and services for 70.1%. Both industry, agriculture, and services are highly developed, with the service sector employing about 37% of the workforce and generating half of the income. Key industries include steel, textiles, food processing, printing, and petrochemicals. The state has mineral resources such as petroleum, bauxite, iron, coal, and marble. Fishing and salt production are thriving. Major agricultural products include sugarcane, citrus fruits, Banana, vegetables, cotton, fruits, and rice, and Livestock is raised. Water, land, and air transportation are well-developed.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro Industrial Zone](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/810a19d8bc3eb135f43067cca41ea8d3fc1f44d6/810a19d8bc3eb135f43067cca41ea8d3fc1f44d6_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro Industrial Zone\n\nIndustrial Strength\n\nRio de Janeiro State is also Brazil's second-largest industrial base. In 2000, there were 22,050 industrial enterprises of various types in the state. It is particularly renowned for its chemical, petrochemical, and pharmaceutical industries, which account for 15% of Brazil's output in these sectors, with offshore oil and mineral extraction leading the way. The state is rich in natural resources, especially offshore oil and natural gas, with reserves and production leading Brazil.\n\n![Petrobras](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/0dd7912397dda1447427c8eab0b7d0a20cf486b8/0dd7912397dda1447427c8eab0b7d0a20cf486b8_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nPetrobras\n\nThe state of Rio de Janeiro has a relatively developed mining and processing industry. Petrobras (Petrobras) ranks first among Brazil's top 100 companies. In addition to oil extraction, it has a large petrochemical plant (Refinaria Duque de Caxias) in Duque de Caxias, Rio de Janeiro State. Brazil's largest mining company, Vale (CVRD), ranks second in Brazil. Large mining enterprises such as Valesul Aluminium also have operations in Rio de Janeiro State. Additionally, there are Companhia Siderurgica Nacional in Volta Redonda, the Cosigua steel plant, the large alkali plant (Fabrica de Alcalis) in Arraial do Cabo, the Volkswagen truck factory (Fabrica de Caminhoes da Volkswagen) in Resende, and others. The processing of marble and granite, and the production of glass, cement, and other building materials, along with the civil construction industry, account for 9.6% of the state's GDP. Shipbuilding and repair, pharmaceuticals and chemicals, plastics, mechanical processing, metal components, printing, and textile and garment manufacturing also hold certain proportions.\n\n![Nuclear Power Plant in Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/adaf2edda3cc7cd96b4490813b01213fb80e9179/adaf2edda3cc7cd96b4490813b01213fb80e9179_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nNuclear Power Plant in Rio de Janeiro\n\nIn addition to three large thermal power plants, Rio de Janeiro State has the Angra 1 and Angra 2 nuclear power plant in the Ilha Grande Bay area. In 2003, the nuclear power plants generated 95 billion kilowatt-hours (9500 GWh) of electricity, which was projected to reach 215 billion kilowatt-hours (21500 GWh) in 2004. Eight additional power generation projects are also planned for construction.\n\nStrength of Agriculture and Animal Husbandry\n\nAgriculture and animal husbandry account for a very small proportion of Rio de Janeiro State's GDP. The Rural population is only 720,000, with approximately 90,000 farming households, primarily focused on supplying the vegetable and non-staple food industry within the state. The agricultural land area is 3,264,150 hectares, generally consisting of farmlands smaller than 10 hectares. Of this agricultural land, 44% is natural pastureland used for grazing dairy cattle. The main crops are Cassava, rice, beans, corn, potatoes, sugarcane, coffee, oranges, bananas, tomatoes, papayas, and other tropical fruits and vegetables. Fishing is also practiced. The northern and northwestern regions primarily produce fruits and sugarcane, with supporting juice and cachaça (sugarcane liquor) processing industries.\n\nMountainous areas primarily produce vegetables and flowers. The southern region produces bananas and tomatoes. The state of Rio de Janeiro has implemented various programs such as Green Currency (Moeda Verde), Orchard Irrigation (Frutificar-Fruticultura Irrigada), Rio-Cafe, Rio-Cana, and Rio-Leite. These programs primarily aim to further promote the development of agriculture and livestock in the region by providing low-interest loans and technical services.\n\nStrength of the Service Sector\n\nThe state of Rio de Janeiro is renowned for its robust service sector, including tourism, information and communications, finance, and insurance. Brazil is one of the world's top 10 tourism revenue earners. Tourists visiting Brazil mostly come from Latin America, the United States, and Europe. The state of Rio de Janeiro is rich in tourism resources and has numerous tourist attractions, with the city of Rio de Janeiro having the most. The most famous landmark is the Christ the Redeemer statue.\n\nThe state of Rio de Janeiro has advanced communications, and its IT industry is also developing rapidly. Its Software Product rank first in Brazil's software industry, accounting for 40% of the Brazilian market. Rede GLOBO, located in Rio, is Brazil's and even Latin America's largest television network. Renowned Brazilian and international financial institutions such as Banco do Brasil (BB), Caixa Econômica Federal (CEF), Banco Nacional de Desenvolvimento Econômico e Social (BNDES), Bradesco, Itaú Unibanco, Santander, Unibanco, ABN AMRO, Bank of Boston, The Hongkong and Shanghai Banking Corporation Limited, and Citibank all have establishments in Rio. The Rio de Janeiro Stock Exchange (Bolsa de Valores) has an annual Volume of 1 billion US dollars.\n\n![Banco do Brasil](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/a686c9177f3e6709e3b3e1a739c79f3df8dc5532/a686c9177f3e6709e3b3e1a739c79f3df8dc5532_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nBanco do Brasil\n\nMajor Ports\n\nThe Port of Rio de Janeiro, the Port of Itaguaí, the Port of Niterói, the Port of Arraial do Cabo, the Port of Angra dos Reis, and the Port of Mangaratiba. Among these, the Port of Itaguaí and the Port of Mangaratiba have been consolidated into the Port of Sepetiba. Except for the Port of Arraial do Cabo, which is managed by the local government, the other four ports are all under the unified management of the Companhia Docas do Rio de Janeiro (Rio de Janeiro State Port Authority).\n\n![Port of Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/f703738da9773912c71d2239fa198618367ae2ab/f703738da9773912c71d2239fa198618367ae2ab_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nPort of Rio de Janeiro\n\nUrban Charm\n\nCarnival\n\nThe local Brazilian Carnival is known as the world's largest and most uninhibited carnival, and Brazil's biggest festival. Its fervor for femininity is unparalleled in the world, attracting millions of domestic and international tourists every year. Among the carnivals held across Brazil, the most renowned is the Rio de Janeiro Carnival, the world's most famous and captivating spectacle. Because the people of Bahia have resisted its commercialization, the carnival here has preserved its authentic flavor, becoming the truest embodiment of Brazilian tradition and the spirit of Carnival. During the festivities, the entire city pours out into the streets. People, regardless of skin color, ethnicity, wealth, or social status, flood the avenues like a tide. Men and women, old and young, all wear heavy makeup, singing and dancing wildly, venting their emotions to the fullest. The grand Samba parade is the climax of the Carnival. Large floats, flanked by the \"King\" and \"Queen\" (former girlfriend of famous footballer Ronaldo, Suzanna, was once chosen as the Carnival \"Queen\" of Rio de Janeiro) lead the way. Voluptuous Latin women in bikinis or topless dance the passionate and unrestrained Samba with male dancers, driving the atmosphere to its peak and making tourists unable to resist joining the reveling crowd. The dazzling costumes, powerful music, fiery Samba dance, and the enchanting beauty of Brazilian women make the experience unforgettable.\n\n![Carnival](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/342ac65c1038534335ca20799113b07eca808865/342ac65c1038534335ca20799113b07eca808865_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nCarnival\n\nSamba\n\nAfricans brought Samba dance, characterized by vigorous shaking of the body to the rhythm of passionate and lively music, with feet moving and spinning rapidly. The mesmerizing dance moves of Samba dancers in dazzling costumes are a sight to behold. The Portuguese introduced the Carnival. Dancing Samba and watching Samba during Carnival are an essential part of Brazilian life. As the \"Capital of Carnival,\" Brazil's most distinctive annual carnival takes place in Rio de Janeiro, with the most representative event being the Samba costume parade, featuring 150,000 performers over five consecutive nights. When night falls, under the escort of countless banners, the Carnival parade participants make their entrance to clear the way, followed by Samba dance performances that set the entire venue ablaze. On ordinary nights, tourists can also choose to visit famous clubs to watch Samba performances.\n\n![Passionate Samba](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/377adab44aed2e73f75321748501a18b87d6fa34/377adab44aed2e73f75321748501a18b87d6fa34_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nPassionate Samba\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/314e251f95cad1c8820b5b7f7d3e6709c93d5131/314e251f95cad1c8820b5b7f7d3e6709c93d5131_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro\n\nCarnival\n\nThe street festivities commence on Friday. The street Carnival attracts thousands of revelers, many dressed as clowns, TV celebrities, or animals. A common sight is men dressed as women; for instance, the Bloco das Piranhas is composed of a group of men deliberately dressed as prostitutes. Another common group is the Bloco dos Sujos, whose members smear themselves with cheap paint and dress as indigenous people or ruffians, parading through the streets.\n\nThe nighttime activities of the Carnival belong to the club balls. Clubs that attract samba dancers and tourists include the Sírio-Libanês and the Flamengo. The Monte Líbano club boasts that its ball is the wildest, especially the Baghdad Night held on Carnival Tuesday. This night's event is extremely popular, sometimes even attracting Muslims from the Middle East to join in the celebration.\n\n![Copacabana Beach](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/d52a2834349b033b9fcb468217ce36d3d539bd23/d52a2834349b033b9fcb468217ce36d3d539bd23_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nCopacabana Beach\n\nAnother highlight of the Carnival is the best costume contests held at several balls, featuring outfits ranging from medieval minstrels to Roman Catholic cardinals.\n\nThe Rio de Janeiro Carnival is the most colorful and vibrant. Undoubtedly the central event of the entire festivities is the large-scale Samba School Parade. Samba is the activity with the strongest Africa influence in the Carnival, blending European folk song styles with African singing and dancing techniques. As for the Samba parade itself, it is a product of the 20th century.\n\n2\n\nDespite its dense population, Rio de Janeiro has not diminished the Latin people's pursuit of pleasure in the slightest. The exuberant Carnival fully showcases Rio's Dionysian culture, but the city's allure extends far beyond this: its beaches, art museums, and cuisine are all reasons why tourists linger and are reluctant to leave.\n\nThe urban area of Rio is divided into North and South zones by the Serra da Carioca mountain range, with the city center (Centro) being the commercial and financial hub where most museums and art galleries are located. The South Zone is the center for tourism and leisure, with the majority of entertainment venues in this area.\n\nFootball Charm\n\nThe Brazilian passion for football is world-renowned. On the streets and beaches, groups of young footballers can be seen everywhere. People from Rio will proudly tell you that world-class players like Arthur Antunes Coimbra, Ronaldo, Ronaldinho, Rivaldo, Romário, Bebeto, Zagallo, and Tostão all hail from Rio. Rio is home to the world's largest football stadium, the Maracana Stadium. The stadium's stands, which once held 150,000 spectators, can still accommodate 105,000 people after renovations. At the stadium's entrance, there are large footprints left by stars such as Zico, Ronaldo, Ronaldinho, and Rivaldo. The Maracanã Stadium has hosted numerous major matches and witnessed many glorious moments in football history. Among them, the legendary Pelé scored his 1,000th goal at this stadium on November 9, 1969. Standing in the stands and imagining the brilliance of these stars, even if you are not a football fan, you might feel a surge of excitement.\n\nCity Stories\n\nThere is a story of unknown origin about Rio's Copacabana Beach. A rich man asked a vagrant lying on the beach sunbathing, \"On such a fine day, why aren't you out at sea fishing?\" The vagrant retorted, \"What for?\" The rich man said, \"You need to catch fish to earn money.\" The vagrant asked, \"What's the point of earning money?\" The rich man said, \"With money, you can buy many things.\" The vagrant asked again, \"And after buying things, what then?\" The rich man said, \"When you have everything you need, you can comfortably lie here and bask in the sun!\" Upon hearing this, the vagrant lazily turned over and said, \"Aren't I already comfortably lying here basking in the sun?\"\n\n![Rio's Beach at Night](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/faedab64034f78f092e020ab7b310a55b2191cfa/faedab64034f78f092e020ab7b310a55b2191cfa_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio's Beach at Night\n\nThis might be a joke, but that's the attitude of Rio's people: leisurely, laid-back, and content with their lot. Indeed, in a city like Rio, blessed with natural advantages, the best of everything—delicious food, beautiful women, stunning scenery—is right at hand.\n\nA 2017 research report released by the Brazilian Public Security Forum showed that in Rio de Janeiro, which had just hosted the Summer Olympics, a total of 38,000 people had died in various violence-related incidents since 2002, averaging as many as 7 people per day.\n\nViolent incidents mainly included gang conflicts, clashes between police and criminals, and injuries from stray bullets. Figures provided by the Brazilian Public Prosecutor's Office showed that of the 6,073 cases where investigations were initiated since 2009, only 150 resulted in indictments, with most cases having no leads.\n\nThe survey indicated that public security in Rio had improved during the first decade of this century, but the number of serious incidents has rebounded in recent years. Statistics from the Rio Civil Police Department show that Rio averages 1,324 fatal incidents per month, of which over 80% were gunshot-related. Most victims' bodies were discovered in remote areas on the city's western outskirts. However, with fewer than 250 personnel in total, including all criminal investigators and forensic experts, conducting on-site investigations has become extremely difficult.\n\n3\n\nThe sidewalks on Rio de Janeiro's beaches are paved with black and white stones, the two most contrasting colors, placed side by side without any ornamentation or transition. This is the Rio way, rejecting compromise and welcoming only the clash of extremes. Interestingly, the outcome of this clash is not the destruction or domination of one side by the other; instead, both sides remain starkly juxtaposed, inseparable yet unmerged. Rio has a population of over ten million, with different skin colors and ethnicities, where people frequently clash over trivial matters, yet religious or racial strife never occurs. Here, extreme poverty and opulent luxury exist side by side. Slums unabashedly perch on hillsides that should be the domain of the wealthy, and penniless homeless people lie next to millionaires on Copacabana Beach, sharing the waves, sun, and sand. In Rio, you can find forests within the city and skyscrapers in the countryside. People constantly complain and demonstrate, yet the joyous Samba in the streets never stops. Mayors come and go, but they cannot change this city. Rio cannot be described with a single word, because its opposite can always be found here.\n\n![Rio's Beaches](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/3ac79f3df8dcd10088284279708b4710b9122f35/3ac79f3df8dcd10088284279708b4710b9122f35_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio's Beaches\n\nTransportation\n\nSince very few people in Brazil own private cars, buses have become the most common means of transportation for the majority of people. Major cities have air-conditioned buses running between residential and commercial areas, with routes from the airport to various bus stations, passing by many large hotels.\n\n![Rio's Subway](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/9f2f070828381f306c302bf1ab014c086e06f0b9/9f2f070828381f306c302bf1ab014c086e06f0b9_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio's Subway\n\nRio de Janeiro has a first-class, extensive subway system. Each station and every train car is equipped with route maps, allowing tourists to travel freely without needing to speak Portuguese. The subway network radiates outward from the city center, with bus lines connecting to the terminal stations. Typically, a single ticket can be used for both the subway and the bus. Trains are not a primary means of transportation in Brazil, as the railway system is not well-developed. However, arranging part of a trip by train is quite popular among tourists because Brazilian trains traverse scenic countryside, and the old-fashioned steam locomotives evoke a sense of nostalgia.\n\nCity Culture\n\nCultural Events\n\nRio de Janeiro is one of the largest cities in South America, but the city is better known for the various cultural celebrations held throughout the year, the most famous of which is Carnival, held two weeks before the traditional Catholic Lenten fasting period.\n\nAt that time, people from all over the world come to Rio de Janeiro to participate in and witness this celebration. Carnival means large crowds, food, color, and, of course, Samba. The Carnival celebration ends on Mardi Gras Tuesday.\n\nIn addition to Carnival, New Year's Eve is also an important festival in Rio de Janeiro, during which several concerts are held and fireworks are set off throughout the city, with the largest display at Copacabana Beach. People wear white clothes, and some perform dances in honor of Yemanjá.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/0ff41bd5ad6eddc47a5f24a03edbb6fd52663334/0ff41bd5ad6eddc47a5f24a03edbb6fd52663334_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro\n\nSports\n\nRio de Janeiro hosted the 2007 Pan American Games and the 5th World Military Games in 2011. Other well-known competitions include the MotoGP Brazilian Grand Prix and the finals of the World Beach Volleyball Championships. Jacarepaguá was the venue for the Brazilian Grand Prix, a Formula Racing race from 1978 to 1990, and for Champ Car events from 1996 to 1999. Rio de Janeiro bid for the 2012 Olympic Summer Games but was unsuccessful. On September 16, 2006, the city announced its bid for the 2016 Olympic Summer Games and was finally selected as the host city on October 2, 2009, becoming the first South American country to host the Olympic Games.\n\n4\n\nSport is one of the popular forms of entertainment. The most popular sport is football. Rio de Janeiro is home to four traditional Brazilian football powerhouses: Botafogo, Flamengo, Fluminense, and Vasco da Gama.\n\nOther popular sports include beach soccer, beach volleyball, basketball, surfing, kitesurfing, hang gliding, motorsports, and Brazilian jiu-jitsu.\n\nRio de Janeiro is also a paradise for rock climbers, with hundreds of climbing spots within the city.\n\nFishing is a popular activity in Brazil. In Rio de Janeiro, there is a place where many Brazilian copper fish, also known as peixe de cobre, can be found. Many markets sell peixes de cobre, which are a staple food in the daily diet of Brazilians.\n\nFrom August 5 to 21, 2016 (Rio local time), the 31st Summer Olympic Games were held in Rio de Janeiro.\n\nOn May 7, 2025, FIFA announced the eight host cities for the 2027 FIFA Women's World Cup in Brazil, with Rio de Janeiro being selected. According to the plan of the Brazilian Women's World Cup Bid Committee, the Maracanã Stadium in Rio de Janeiro will host both the opening match and the final. This will mark the third time this stadium hosts a World Cup final (the previous two were the 1950 and 2014 men's World Cup finals). On May 27 local time, the 2025 ITTF Congress was held in Doha, Qatar, where the host cities for the 2027-2029 World Table Tennis Championships were selected. Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, was awarded the hosting rights for the 2029 ITTF World Table Tennis Championships.\n\n15\n\nCulinary Culture\n\nBrazilian cuisine is known for its robust flavors. Bahian cuisine is particularly famous for being Mala. Most Brazilians enjoy red Chili pepper (Pimenta), and the local malagueta pepper is often used generously. The signature dish of Brazil is Feijoada. In Rio de Janeiro, having Feijoada for weekend lunch has become a local tradition. The complete meal (Completa) includes rice, finely shredded Cabbage (Couve), toasted cassava flour (farofa), and sliced Liu Cheng orange. Among Brazilian dishes, Bahian cuisine offers the most distinctive flavors. Bahians have a fondness for chili peppers, and many of their dishes incorporate ingredients such as peanuts, Cashew, and Peeled, dried sea shrimp.\n\nThe most famous dishes from Bahia include:\n\nVatapá (a thick stew made with fresh shrimp, dried shrimp, fresh fish, peanuts, coconut milk, dende oil, seasonings, and bread);\n\n5\n\nMoqueca (a stew made with fresh fish, shrimp, crab, or assorted seafood, cooked with dende oil and coconut milk sauce);\n\n6\n\nXinxim de Galinha (diced chicken stir-fried with dende oil, dried shrimp, and peanuts);\n\nCaruru (fresh shrimp stir-fried with okra and dende oil);\n\nBobó de Camarão (a dish whose seasonings include cassava puree, fresh shrimp, dende oil, and coconut milk);\n\nAcarajé (a small fritter made from black-eyed pea dough, deep-fried in dende oil, and filled with Vatapá, dried shrimp, and red chili pepper).\n\n7\n\nTourist Attractions\n\nRio is renowned worldwide for its stunning landscape of mountains and water and its vibrant cultural life, and has also been rated as one of the \"50 most worthwhile places to visit in the world\". This enchanting city of Rio captivates visitors and makes them reluctant to leave!\n\n![Tourist Map of Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/020e66f09405d09c7931aab0/020e66f09405d09c7931aab0_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nTourist Map of Rio de Janeiro\n\nBeaches\n\nFor the people of Rio, beaches are not just a part of life; they are life itself. The beaches serve simultaneously as a clinic, campus, library, football field, volleyball court, singles bar, dance hall, rock concert venue, sports center, and even an office for the locals. Although some people occasionally go into the sea for Bubble Water , this is merely a brief leisurely interlude in the beach life of Rio's residents. The rest of the time, they read, chat, flirt, jog, exercise, nap, contemplate, and even conduct business on the sand.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/aec379310a55b3191cf1b53e41a98226cffc1774/aec379310a55b3191cf1b53e41a98226cffc1774_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\n![Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/72f082025aafa40fa536fa96a964034f79f0198c/72f082025aafa40fa536fa96a964034f79f0198c_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro Beach\n\nWalking along the beach, one sees the deep blue waters and rolling waves, feels the fine, golden sand underfoot, hears the gentle sea breeze, and can smell the distinct scent of the sea carried by the wind. A broad seaside avenue winds along the coastline. The sidewalks of this avenue are inlaid with white and black pebbles forming various wave-like patterns. Tall, straight palm trees lining the sidewalks sway gently in the sea breeze. On the other side of the avenue stand rows of hotels, restaurants, and luxury apartments, each twenty to thirty stories high. The modern architecture blends harmoniously with the beautiful seaside scenery, complementing each other perfectly. Both day and night, the beaches are filled with people coming to stroll, play football, sunbathe, and surf in the waves. Every weekend, the eight-kilometer stretch of beach becomes a sea of colorful umbrellas, bustling with activity and crowds of people!\n\nSugarloaf Mountain\n\nThe beauty of Rio lies first and foremost in its mountains. The mountains embracing Rio are spectacular with their towering, uniquely shaped peaks. Perennially green, the mountains are lush and layered with beautiful scenery. Among them, Sugarloaf Mountain and Corcovado Mountain are must-visit destinations for tourists from around the world. Sugarloaf Mountain majestically stands between the bay and the Atlantic Ocean. The mountain is steep with smooth walls, nearly four hundred meters high. Viewed from a distance, it stands isolated between the vast sky and earth, playfully called \"Pão de Açúcar\" (Sugarloaf) by Brazilians.\n\n![Sugarloaf Mountain in Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/fc1f4134970a304e042d06a2d3c8a786c9175c5e/fc1f4134970a304e042d06a2d3c8a786c9175c5e_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nSugarloaf Mountain in Rio de Janeiro\n\nTaking the cable car to the top of Sugarloaf Mountain and looking out from the railing, the cityscape of Rio de Janeiro unfolds in its entirety: on one side lies Guanabara Bay, where white yachts and sailboats are dotted across the azure waters. The semicircular Botafogo Beach features fine golden sand and gentle waves, flanked by a seaside boulevard lined with layers of orderly, well-proportioned high-rise buildings.\n\nIn the distance, a long dragon-like structure spans the two sides of the bay—the famous Niterói Bridge. This bridge, with a total length of 14 kilometers, connects Rio with the city of Niterói on the opposite shore. On the other side lies the vast Atlantic Ocean, its sparkling surface occasionally traversed by massive ocean liners. Here and there, islands of various shapes appear and disappear amidst the waves and tides. In the distance, Copacabana Beach lies like a crescent moon beside the Atlantic, with colorful cars coming and going from all directions, resembling countless Beetle crawling slowly along the crescent-shaped coastal avenue...\n\n![Niterói Bridge](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/5882b2b7d0a20cf4ed88588774094b36acaf994a/5882b2b7d0a20cf4ed88588774094b36acaf994a_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nNiterói Bridge\n\nAscending Corcovado, a different view unfolds before one's eyes. At the summit, more than 700 meters above sea level, stands a huge statue of Jesus, 38 meters tall and weighing 1,145 tons, one of the world's giant sculptures. The statue of Jesus stands upright with arms outstretched, like a huge cross on the mountaintop, symbolizing that Brazil is a Catholic country.\n\n20\n\nCorcovado Mountain (Jesus Mountain)\n\nCorcovado Mountain is a symbol of Rio. When visiting Salvador, another place not to be missed is the beautiful and mysterious \"Abaete Lake,\" also known as the Black Lake. The water of the Black Lake is a mysterious, gleaming black, while the sand on its shore is as white as snow, dazzlingly bright. The two reflect each other, making the white appear whiter and the black blacker, creating a beauty that is breathtaking.\n\nThe statue of Jesus Christ on Corcovado Mountain in Rio is strategically positioned. As you stroll through the city, you can see the statue on the mountain from everywhere: it looks down, with arms outstretched, as if watching over the city of Rio at its feet. At night, illuminated by lights, the statue appears even taller and more prominent against the dark backdrop, intensifying its religious aura. Corcovado Mountain is high and often foggy. Even under a bright, sunny sky, thick fog can suddenly roll in, enveloping the entire mountaintop in an instant, creating a misty, dreamlike world. Being there feels ethereal, as if entering a fairyland.\n\n![View of Rio de Janeiro from Corcovado Mountain](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/9922720e0cf3d7cad7a0992ef01fbe096b63a910/9922720e0cf3d7cad7a0992ef01fbe096b63a910_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nView of Rio de Janeiro from Corcovado Mountain\n\nRio de Janeiro is beautiful not only for its mountains but also for its scenery. Walking down from the mountains, besides the large freshwater lake in the city center, beaches of various sizes line the coast one after another, like a dazzling, crystal-clear necklace adorning Rio's \"neck,\" exuding an alluring charm. The most famous is Copacabana Beach, a crescent-shaped beach over a hundred meters wide.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro Christ the Redeemer](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/86d6277f9e2f070899a67dbcec24b899a801f2ef/86d6277f9e2f070899a67dbcec24b899a801f2ef_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nRio de Janeiro Christ the Redeemer\n\nChrist the Redeemer Statue\n\nThe Christ the Redeemer statue ( Portuguese : Cristo Redentor) is a large Art Deco-style statue of Jesus Christ, located in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil. It is a symbol of the city and one of the world's most famous monumental sculptures, selected in 2007.\n\n![Christ the Redeemer 1](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/d089b98690ba325266096e3e/d089b98690ba325266096e3e_3_4?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto)\n\nChrist the Redeemer 1\n\n![Christ the Redeemer 2](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/c28fddfd96aedf29d7887d3e/c28fddfd96aedf29d7887d3e_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nChrist the Redeemer 2\n\n![Christ the Redeemer](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/562c11dfa9ec8a1378646e9ef403918fa0ecc055/562c11dfa9ec8a1378646e9ef403918fa0ecc055_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_400,limit_1)\n\nChrist the Redeemer\n\nThe Christ the Redeemerstatue has been standing atop Corcovado Mountain in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, for over 75 years. It weighs 1,145 metric tons, stands 38 meters tall, with outstretched arms spanning 28 meters. It was designed by the Rzeczpospolita Polska -French artist Paul Landovsky. Between 1926 and 1931, he first created the statue segments in France, which were then shipped to Brazil for assembly. Constructed primarily from reinforced concrete, the project took four years to complete and is considered one of Brazil's most famous landmarks, visible from almost every angle within Rio de Janeiro! The Christ the Redeemer statue is located atop Corcovado Mountain in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, and its pedestal also houses a Catholic chapel capable of accommodating 150 people.\n\nThe idea of erecting a statue on Corcovado Mountain originated in the mid-1850s, when a Catholic priest, Pedro Maria Boss, requested funding from Princess Isabel of the Empire of Brazil to build a large religious monument. Princess Isabel did not pay much attention to the idea, and when Brazil became a republic in 1889, the project was completely dismissed by the government due to laws enforcing the Separation of church and state .\n\nA second proposal \"to build a landmark on the mountain\" was put forward by the Archbishop of Rio de Janeiro in 1921. The archbishop organized an event called \"Monument Week\" (Semana do Monumento) to attract donations, which came primarily from Brazilian Catholics. The design requirements for the Christ statue included: it must represent the Christian cross, feature a statue of Jesus Christ holding a globe, and have a pedestal symbolizing the world. Finally, the design of \"Christ the Redeemerwith outstretched arms\" was chosen.\n\nThis commemorative statue was designed by French monument sculptor Paul Landowski, with construction overseen by local engineer Heitor da Silva Costa. A team of engineers and technicians studied Landowski's design proposal and decided to use reinforced concrete instead of steel, as it was more suitable for the cross-shaped statue. Costa and Landowski chose Talcum as the outer layer material for the statue due to its high malleability and resistance to harsh weather conditions. Rio de Janeiro also specifically constructed a railway up Corcovado Mountain to transport the large stone blocks needed for creating the statue to the summit.\n\nOn October 12, 1931, a grand inauguration ceremony for the Christ statue was held on Corcovado Mountain. President of Brazil Getúlio Vargas cut the ribbon for the statue. This day coincided with the feast day of Brazil's patron saint, Our Lady of Aparecida—Our Lady of Aparecida Day (also known as the Day of the Patron Saint). A highlight of the inauguration was the activation of the lighting system. It was originally planned for Italian inventor Guglielmo Marconi to activate the switch via radio from his yacht in Naples , using his invention. However, due to severe weather conditions that day which affected signal intensity , it ultimately had to be activated manually by workers on Corcovado Mountain.\n\n![Vargas](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/a08b87d6277f9e2f85b4bb501d30e924b999f3e1/a08b87d6277f9e2f85b4bb501d30e924b999f3e1_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nVargas\n\nOn October 12, 2006, during the 75th-anniversary celebration of the statue's completion, Cardinal Archbishop of Rio de Janeiro, Eusébio Oscar Scheid, celebrated a mass for Our Lady of Aparecida Day beneath the statue. This event established the Christ statue as a pilgrimage site, and from then on, Catholics could receive baptisms and declare marriages in front of the statue.\n\n8\n\nNational Museum of Brazil\n\nThe palace located within the Quinta da Boa Vista was designated as the National Museum of Brazil, housing a collection of over one million items. These include various weapons, clothing, and daily utensils used by the ancient indigenous peoples of Latin America, the Indians, as well as thousands of Brazilian mineral and animal specimens, and historical documents from various periods. Among the most valuable items is the world's largest meteorite, weighing 5,360 kilograms, discovered in the state of Bahia in the northeast. The museum also holds a human skull unearthed in Brazil in 1975. After examination, scientists believe this skull dates back over 10,000 years, making it the oldest human fossil found in the Americas.\n\n![National Museum of Brazil](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/c75c10385343fbf27f3a077bb27eca8065388f50/c75c10385343fbf27f3a077bb27eca8065388f50_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nNational Museum of Brazil\n\nOn the streets of downtown Rio, European-style buildings from the colonial period stand in harmony with modern skyscrapers. The majestic palaces, solemn churches, and ornately decorated grand theaters... most of these ancient structures are well-preserved, with some being converted into memorial halls or museums.\n\n9\n\nShopping Centers\n\nCompared to the capital Brasília , Rio de Janeiro aligns more closely with the common perception of a metropolis. The commercial streets lined with shops, over 30 large shopping malls, and open-air markets in Rio de Janeiro all feel very familiar. Specialty items such as jewelry and football T-shirt are among the city's shopping highlights.\n\n![Waterfront Promenade](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/a54e55fbd8aa1024034f56ac/a54e55fbd8aa1024034f56ac_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nWaterfront Promenade\n\nThe famous beach Copacabana and its surrounding neighborhoods are the most prosperous commercial area in Rio de Janeiro. In addition to many specialty stores, there are also bookstores, souvenir shops, and various other stores. The neighborhood around the other major beach, Ipanema, is Rio de Janeiro's most fashionable commercial district, with many high-end clothing stores lining the streets. The Leblon neighborhood is also an important district of Rio de Janeiro.\n\nRio de Janeiro has a large number of shopping malls and open-air markets, with a full range of commodities and relatively convenient transportation, which can meet the shopping needs of local residents and tourists.\n\nThe Avenida Atlntica neighborhood is a key place to purchase Brazilian gemstones and gold jewelry. On weekend evenings, vendors sell handicrafts and clothing such as pottery, paintings, sculptures, and hammocks here. The Praça General Osório in the Ipanema neighborhood hosts the Hippie Fair every Sunday from 8:00 to 18:00, attracting many tourists and selling handicrafts, leather goods, clothing, jewelry, and various souvenirs. The weekend market Babilônia Hype Fair gathers over 150 stalls selling art and decorative items, with performers providing music and dance entertainment. The Northeastern Market (Feira Nordestina), featuring street performers like musicians and magicians, is located at Campo de São Cristóvão and opens every Sunday.\n\nBest Time to Visit\n\nSpring and autumn are the best times to visit Brazil. Brazil is located in the Southern Hemisphere, so its seasons are opposite to those of the Northern Hemisphere. Summer lasts from late December to late March, and winter from late June to late September. The local summer is the peak tourist season. To avoid crowds and temperatures above 37 degrees Celsius, the period from April to October is a better time.\n\nImportant Notes\n\n1. No hotels in Brazil provide disposable slippers, toothpaste, or toothbrushes for guests. Travelers must prepare these items themselves.\n\n2. Petty theft is relatively common in Brazil. Be especially cautious when boarding or disembarking from buses. Additionally, avoid going out alone while shopping or at night. Passports, valuables, and cash should be stored in the hotel safe rather than carried on your person to prevent theft.\n\n3. The major cities in Brazil generally have good sanitary conditions, and there are no widespread epidemic diseases. However, it should be noted that tap water contains impurities and rust, so it is advisable to avoid drinking it untreated. For those planning to visit the Amazon region or the Pantanal wetlands, it is recommended to get a yellow fever vaccination for safety reasons.\n\n4. Tipping: Most restaurants typically add a 10% service charge to the bill. If the service is exceptionally good, you may consider giving the waiter an additional tip. At airports, tip porters and drivers who assist with luggage, with an amount around 0.5 US dollars. Hairdressers and beauticians should receive a 10% to 20% tip. Street kids who watch your car will ask for about 0.5 US dollars when you return. If you park outside a busy nightclub or theater, these car watchers may ask for 1 or 1.5 US dollars in advance, and it is best to pay.\n\nLeisure and Entertainment\n\nThe nighttime festivities of the Carnival belong to club balls. Clubs that attract samba dancers and tourists include the lavish parties held by the Sirio-Libanes, Flamengo, Fluminense, and Monte-Libao clubs. Another highlight of the Carnival is the best costume contests held at several balls, featuring various costumes such as that of a Roman Catholic archbishop.\n\n2\n\nTravel Tips\n\nLanguage: Portugueseis the official language of Brazil, and most people do not speak foreign languages. However, English is also commonly used in large hotels and restaurants. Additionally, Español is relatively common, and most people can understand Spanish.\n\n![A Distant View of Rio de Janeiro](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/bf4875638164d0200c33faa6/bf4875638164d0200c33faa6_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nA Distant View of Rio de Janeiro\n\nClimate: Brazil is located in the Southern Hemisphere. Winter lasts from June to August, with average temperatures around 13°C to 18°C, and it occasionally snows.\n\nCurrency: The República Federativa do Brasil ian currency is called the \"Real.\" Due to frequent fluctuations in the República Federativa do Brasil exchange rate, it is not advisable for tourists to exchange too much money upon arrival; it's better to exchange as needed. Most hotels and bars in Brazil include a 10% service charge in the bill, so tipping is not necessary. Taxi drivers in Brazil do not expect tips, while airport and hotel porters generally receive a tip of 1 US dollar per piece of luggage.\n\nTime Difference: During the summer in Brazil, the time is 10 hours behind Beijing; during the winter, it is 11 hours behind Beijing.\n\nTransportation: Brazil's railway system is not well-developed, and the primary mode of transportation is by bus. Major cities have air-conditioned buses running between residential and commercial areas, with routes connecting the airport to various bus stations. Ordinary buses typically have passengers board from the rear door and alight from the front door. Rio de Janeiro and São Paulo have first-class subway systems, which are convenient and fast for tourists to use.\n\nShopping: Items worth purchasing in Brazil include coffee and gemstones. Brazil is a coffee production kingdom, where coffee is of good quality and inexpensive. Brazil boasts a wide variety of gemstones, such as amethyst, Opal , and Topaz , with over half of the world's Colored Gemstone produced in Brazil. Additionally, propolis, leather goods, and handicrafts are also excellent choices for gifts.\n\nSister cities\n\nBeijing, China\n\n10\n\nWenzhou, China\n\n18\n\nCabo Frio, Brazil\n\nTeresópolis, Brazil\n\nNiterói, Brazil\n\nSantos, Brazil\n\nBarcelona, Spain\n\nBuenos Aires, República Argentina\n\nCape Town, South Africa\n\nDurban, South Africa\n\nJeddah, Kingdom of Saudi Arabia\n\nLisbon, Portugal\n\nLos Angeles, United States\n\nMiami, United States\n\nAtlanta, United States\n\nMontreal, Canada\n\nOsaka, Japan\n\nParis, France\n\nSeoul, South Korea\n\nSydney, Australia\n\nReal-time News\n\nThe 2017 Brazil Rio Carnival is in full swing.\n\nThe Rio Samba venue covers approximately 85,000 square meters. During the Carnival period, five samba events will be held at the venue, with over a hundred samba schools competing for the top spot. Subsequently, the top five schools will once again present a spectacular samba visual feast with their brilliant dance skills for the city's audience.\n\nPersistent heatwave sweeps across multiple areas in southeastern Brazil.\n\nOn February 19, 2025 local time, a press conference was held in Rio de Janeiro State. Antonio de Sales, head of the fire department, stated that due to the ongoing influence of the heatwave, the number of forest fires in Rio de Janeiro State has increased by 370% compared to the same period last year. On February 17 alone, 312 forest fires were recorded within the state. From early January to February 17, the fire department has recorded more than 2,000 forest fires.\n\nBid Process\n\nDuring the Pan American Games held in early 2007, Rio de Janeiro formally submitted a special letter to the International Olympic Committee applying to host the 2016 Summer Olympics. This was Rio de Janeiro's third bid to host the Olympic Games, following two failed attempts for the 2004 and 2012 Games. The other cities ultimately confirmed as candidates for the 2016 Olympics were: Baku (The Republic of Azerbaijan), Chicago (United States), Doha (The State of Qatar), Madrid (Spain), Prague (Česká republika), and Tokyo (Japan). In the early hours of October 3, 2009, Beijing standard time, during the 121st IOC Session in Copenhagen, The Kingdom of Denmark, Rio de Janeiro defeated Madrid (Spain), Chicago (United States), and Tokyo (Japan) through three rounds of voting, ultimately winning the right to host the 31st Summer Olympic Games in 2016. República Federativa do Brasil became the first South America country to host the Olympic Games.\n\n![Brazilian Officials Celebrate Rio's Successful Olympic Bid](https://gbaike-image.cdn.bcebos.com/902397dda144ad3480323ec5d8a20cf430ad85e7/902397dda144ad3480323ec5d8a20cf430ad85e7_url?x-bce-process=image/format,f_auto/resize,m_lfit,h_1024,limit_1)\n\nBrazilian Officials Celebrate Rio's Successful Olympic Bid\n\n1.\n\n巴西第二大城市——里约热内卢 . 新华网 . Optimize Airport Ground Transit\n\nTaxis from distant hubs can consume 20% of your transit budget. Research express train passes before landing.\n\n#### 4. Travel Budgeting: The 2026 Algorithmic Cost Framework\n\nStatistical Macroeconomic Modeling, Purchasing Power Parity (PPP), and Multi-Tier Expense Planning\n\n📊\n\nExecutive Summary & Overview Calculating a realistic international travel budget requires navigating complex macroeconomic variables rather than relying on static estimates. In 2026, global inflation, foreign exchange volatility, and surge pricing in the hospitality sector have reshaped the cost of overseas exploration. Our Travel Cost Calculator bridges statistical accuracy and real-world travel logistics by combining live interbank exchange rates, World Bank International Comparison Program (ICP) datasets, Eurostat consumer price indices (CPI), and crowd-verified on-the-ground expense logs across 195+ countries and 500+ cities.\n\n### 1. The Anatomy of Modern Travel Costs: The 4-Pillar Model\n\nEvery destination budget fundamentally breaks down into four core spending categories: (1) Accommodation, which accounts for 40% to 55% of baseline daily expenditure; (2) Food and Dining, representing 20% to 30% depending on dining style; (3) Local Urban Transit, making up 10% to 15%; and (4) Sightseeing, Attractions, and Incidental Culture, accounting for 10% to 20%. Our mathematical engine calculates these pillars independently using localized cost-of-living multipliers, ensuring that a surge in hotel rates (e.g., during summer in Amsterdam) does not distort base food and metro estimates.\n\n### 2. Multi-Tier Budget Architecture: Backpacker, Standard, and Luxury\n\nTravelers operate under vastly different financial parameters. Our engine models three standardized tiers:\n\n• Backpacker Tier: Modeled around licensed hostel dorm beds ($15–$45/night in Europe, $5–$15 in Southeast Asia), hyper-local street food and grocery market cooking, public bus/subway networks, and free walking tours.\n\n• Standard / Mid-Range Tier: Designed for the typical independent traveler, incorporating private 3-star boutique hotels or studio apartments ($80–$180/night), casual sit-down bistros, mixed public transit and occasional rideshares, and paid landmark entry passes.\n\n• Luxury Tier: Engineered for premium comfort, tracking 4-star to 5-star international hotel chains and luxury villas ($250–$700+/night), multi-course fine dining with wine pairings, private airport chauffeur transfers, and skip-the-line VIP guided museum experiences.\n\n### 3. Managing Foreign Currency Volatility & Dynamic Conversion Traps\n\nOne of the most insidious drains on any travel fund is the payment method itself. Dynamic Currency Conversion (DCC)—where a foreign card terminal or ATM asks if you want to be charged in your home currency—adds an arbitrary 3% to 8% merchant markup on top of the interbank exchange rate. Always select the local native currency when swiping cards or withdrawing cash abroad. Pairing this habit with multi-currency debit accounts (such as Wise or Revolut) effectively saves $150 to $400 on a typical two-week international vacation.\n\n### 4. The 50/30/20 Travel Allocation Blueprint\n\nTo safeguard against unexpected mid-trip financial stress, professional travel planners recommend the 50/30/20 budgeting rule: Allocate 50% of your total projected holiday fund to non-negotiable fixed logistical costs (international flights, intercity trains, hotel bookings), 30% to flexible daily discretionary spending (dinners, nightlife, souvenirs, optional excursions), and retain 20% in an untouched liquid emergency buffer to handle medical copays, flight rescheduling fees, or emergency taxi rides.\n\n💡\n\n### Frequently Asked Questions & Practical Advice\n\n#### Q: How often are the destination cost databases updated for 2026?\n\nOur team conducts monthly dataset synchronizations incorporating official national statistical agency updates (TÜİK, Eurostat, US Bureau of Labor Statistics), verified Numbeo and Expatistan price points, and seasonal hotel aggregator pricing shifts.\n\n#### Q: Does the daily cost calculator include international airfare?\n\nNo. The main calculator strictly models ground living expenses in your chosen destination. Because flight pricing depends heavily on your point of origin, ticket class, and booking lead time, we recommend pairing this tool with our dedicated Flight Cost Estimator for complete end-to-end trip projections.\n\n#### Q: How does traveling with a partner or group reduce the per-person daily cost?\n\nAccommodation represents the largest fixed single-traveler penalty. When sharing double rooms, rental apartments, or split taxi rides, couples and groups routinely achieve a 20% to 35% per-person reduction compared to solo travelers paying full room rates.\n\n#### Q: How do seasonal shoulder months impact overall destination expenses?\n\nTraveling during shoulder season (e.g., May or October in Southern Europe, or April and November in Japan) lowers hotel and flight prices by 30% to 50% compared to peak July/August without sacrificing favorable sightseeing weather.\n\nWe Value Your Privacy\n\nWe use cookies to personalize content, support site features, and analyze traffic in compliance with GDPR. Read our [Privacy Policy](/privacy).\n\n ",
